T 3 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java punctuation.definition.comment.java
L  * Static convenience methods that help a method or constructor check whether it was invoked
T 92 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * correctly (that is, whether its <i>preconditions</i> were met).
T 66 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>If the precondition is not met, the {@code Preconditions} method throws an unchecked exception
T 100 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * of a specified type, which helps the method in which the exception was thrown communicate that
T 97 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * its caller has made a mistake. This allows constructs such as
T 64 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * {@snippet :
T 14 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * public static double sqrt(double value) {
T 44 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *   if (value < 0) {
T 21 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *     throw new IllegalArgumentException("input is negative: " + value);
T 73 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *   }
T 6 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *   // calculate square root
T 29 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * }
T 4 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * }
T 4 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>to be replaced with the more compact
T 42 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * {@snippet :
T 14 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * public static double sqrt(double value) {
T 44 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *   checkArgument(value >= 0, "input is negative: %s", value);
T 63 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *   // calculate square root
T 29 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * }
T 4 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * }
T 4 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>so that a hypothetical bad caller of this method, such as:
T 64 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * {@snippet :
T 14 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * void exampleBadCaller() {
T 28 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *   double d = sqrt(-1.0);
T 27 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * }
T 4 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * }
T 4 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>would be flagged as having called {@code sqrt()} with an illegal argument.
T 80 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <h2>Performance</h2>
T 23 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>Avoid passing message arguments that are expensive to compute; your code will always compute
T 98 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * them, even though they usually won't be needed. If you have such arguments, use the conventional
T 99 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * if/throw idiom instead.
T 26 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>Depending on your message arguments, memory may be allocated for boxing and varargs array
T 95 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * creation. However, the methods of this class have a large number of overloads that prevent such
T 98 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * allocations in many common cases.
T 36 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>The message string is not formatted unless the exception will be thrown, so the cost of the
T 97 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * string formatting itself should not be a concern.
T 52 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>As with any performance concerns, you should consider profiling your code (in a production
T 96 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * environment if possible) before spending a lot of effort on tweaking a particular element.
T 93 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <h2>Other types of preconditions</h2>
T 40 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>Not every type of precondition failure is supported by these methods. Continue to throw
T 93 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * standard JDK exceptions such as {@link java.util.NoSuchElementException} or {@link
T 85 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * UnsupportedOperationException} in the situations they are intended for.
T 74 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <h2>Non-preconditions</h2>
T 29 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>It is of course possible to use the methods of this class to check for invalid conditions
T 95 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * which are <i>not the caller's fault</i>. Doing so is <b>not recommended</b> because it is
T 92 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * misleading to future readers of the code and of stack traces. See <a
T 71 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * href="https://github.com/google/guava/wiki/ConditionalFailuresExplained">Conditional failures
T 96 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * explained</a> in the Guava User Guide for more advice. Notably, {@link Verify} offers assertions
T 99 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * similar to those in this class for non-precondition checks.
T 62 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <h2>{@code java.util.Objects.requireNonNull()}</h2>
T 54 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>Projects which use {@code com.google.common} should generally avoid the use of {@link
T 91 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * Objects#requireNonNull(Object)}. Instead, use whichever of {@link #checkNotNull(Object)} or
T 62 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 63 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 68 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java keyword.other.documentation.javadoc.java
T 70 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 90 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java variable.parameter.java
T 91 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 94 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * {@link Verify#verifyNotNull(Object)} is appropriate to the situation. (The same goes for the
T 3 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 4 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 9 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java keyword.other.documentation.javadoc.java
T 10 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 16 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java entity.name.type.class.java
T 17 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 38 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java variable.parameter.java
T 39 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
T 95 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * message-accepting overloads.)
T 32 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <h2>Only {@code %s} is supported</h2>
T 40 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>{@code Preconditions} uses {@link Strings#lenientFormat} to format error message template
T 95 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * strings. This only supports the {@code "%s"} specifier, not the full range of {@link
T 87 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * java.util.Formatter} specifiers. However, note that if the number of arguments does not match the
T 100 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * number of occurrences of {@code "%s"} in the format string, {@code Preconditions} will still
T 95 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * behave as expected, and will still include all argument values in the error message; the message
T 99 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * will simply not be formatted exactly as intended.
T 52 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <h2>More information</h2>
T 28 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  *
T 2 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * <p>See the Guava User Guide on <a
T 36 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * href="https://github.com/google/guava/wiki/PreconditionsExplained">using {@code
T 82 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java
L  * Preconditions}</a>.
T 22 source.java comment.block.javadoc.java

L    * Preconditions.checkNotNull is *intended* for performing eager null checks on parameters that a
T 99 source.java meta.class.java meta.class.body.java comment.block.java
L    * nullness checker can already "prove" are non-null. That means that the first parameter to
T 94 source.java meta.class.java meta.class.body.java comment.block.java
L    * checkNotNull *should* be annotated to require it to be non-null.
T 69 source.java meta.class.java meta.class.body.java comment.block.java

T 4 source.java meta.class.java meta.class.body.java comment.block.java
L    * However, for a variety of reasons, Google developers have written a ton of code over the past
T 98 source.java meta.class.java meta.class.body.java comment.block.java
L    * decade that assumes that they can use checkNotNull for non-precondition checks. I had hoped to
T 99 source.java meta.class.java meta.class.body.java comment.block.java
L    * take a principled stand on this, but the amount of such code is simply overwhelming. To avoid
T 98 source.java meta.class.java meta.class.body.java comment.block.java
L    * creating a lot of compile errors that users would not find to be informative, we're giving in
T 98 source.java meta.class.java meta.class.body.java comment.block.java
L    * and allowing callers to pass arguments that a nullness checker believes could be null.
T 91 source.java meta.class.java meta.class.body.java comment.block.java
L    *
T 4 source.java meta.class.java meta.class.body.java comment.block.java
L    * We still encourage people to use requireNonNull over checkNotNull for non-precondition checks.
